![](https://i.ormos.uk/b7/ba/b7ba377dea68dffd01b70710953677f4b8a9b770e584c9b170be0d12cc7cf9eb.jpg)

M-Fest is at an end!

We have had a marvellous festival of **Magic** this weekend, capped off by a Great British Nationals with a top eight of incredibly high quality. While no Scottish players made it through to Sunday's play, Both England and Wales were well represented, with top names like Nick Lovett, Stuart Wright, Dave Grant and Craig Jones all forming part of a star studded ensemble.

The story was predominantly about the beatdown, with 3 Rakdos decks, a Green/Black Rack deck, Red Deck Wins 2007 all represented. A Dredge deck, Perilous Storm and an Aethermage's Touch deck made it eight.

All weekend Craig had declared Red Spells to be the best. Prof's deck, prophetically named Red Deck Wins 2007 in spite of the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)s and [Troll Ascetic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Troll+Ascetic)s was dedicated to the master, Dan Paskins, and burned all it's foes to a crisp. Leading one of the strongest National teams in years, Craig Jones' plan is now to take on New York at Worlds.

**Congratulations to Craig Jones, the Great Britain National Champion, and to Stuart Wright, Marco Orsini-Jones and Dan Godfrey the GB National Team!**

## Top 8 Player profiles

 

Stuart Wright  
 Age: 24  
 Occupation: **Magic** Player  
 From: Frimley, England  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: Top 16 PT New Orleans Top 16 Worlds Top 8 English Nats 2006  
 Record in Standard: 5-1-1  
 Record in Draft: 5-2  
 What deck did you play in Standard and why? Dredge, because it seemed like the best deck  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? I think it's quite interesting, but it might be hard for some players to get the cards for legacy decks. There are a lot of obscure expensive cards that you might theoretically need.

Marco Orsini-Jones  
 Age: 18  
 Occupation: Student  
 From: Coventry, England  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: I qualified for Valencia with a deck I picked up at the last minute. I didn't test much for this either. My recommendation to people… don't test!  
 Record in Standard: 5-1-1  
 Record in Draft: 5-2  
 What deck did you play in Standard and why? Black/Green Rack, because Stuart Walker, who built the deck, and tested it with Max Carey, told me it was good.  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? I've never played it before. I'll probably get a deck at the last minute and see how it goes.

Eduardo Sajgalik  
 Age: 19  
 Occupation: Chemistry Student  
 From: Paris, France originally, but now Bristol, England  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: This is my first Nationals. I played at Pro Tour Geneva this year too.  
 Record in Standard: 5-1-1  
 Record in Draft: 5-2  
 What deck did you play in Standard and why? I played the Blink/Touch deck, with a modified sideboard, taking out some bad cards, and replacing them with better ones. It seemed to be the deck of the moment, without any awful matchups, and plenty of opportunity to outplay your opponent. I would rather have a deck that supports play skill.  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? I already play Legacy a bit, but I'm worried a bit about card availability. Legacy also seems like a format more based on matchups than most at the moment, so it should be pretty interesting.

Craig Jones  
 Age: Not telling… I'm the oldest one in the top eight. Look at my Pro Player card if you want to work it out  
 Occupation: PhD Student  
 From: Manchester, England  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: Winner GP Birmingham 1998, Runner Up PT Honolulu 2006, 1 PT top 16, 2 PT top 32. National team twice. I've also beaten Kai on a Sunday.  
 Record in Standard: 5-1-1  
 Record in Draft: 5-2  
 What deck did you play in Standard and why? Red Deck Wins with green spells. It's probably the best deck in an open field and I know it the best.  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? I can see why it's probably the only format they can run because PT Valencia is Extended just before New York. It's going to be pretty tough to do last minute deck changes, and it seems like it could be a little unfair on countries where there aren't a lot of dual lands around. I've already got mine though (cackles maniacally).

Dave 'The Great White Hype' Grant  
 Age: 28  
 Occupation: Systems Consultant  
 From: Guilford, England  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: Top 4 GP Birmingham in *Mirrodin* block, English National Team once  
 Record in Standard: 4-2-1  
 Record in Draft: 6-1 (I should have 7-0'd)  
 What deck did you play in Standard and why? Rakdos, because it meant that I didn't have to think  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? Legacy? What is Legacy again…?

Simon O'Keeffe  
 Age: 27  
 Occupation: Data Analyst  
 From: Dublin originally, currently residing in London, England  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: I played at PT Prague and PT Charleston and won the Keith Loveys Memorial Trophy  
 Record in Standard: 5-0-2 - I only lost 2 games all weekend  
 Record in Draft: 5-2  
 What deck did you play in Standard and why? Rakdos. Originally we tested Gruul a lot, and we thought it was really good. Then we saw that Rakdos was just better. Also, this deck has [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), who is bloody nuts.  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? I would love it. I play more Legacy than any other format, and I think I only have three losses lifetime in sanctioned Legacy. It's great fun. I hope the seasoned Pros don't ruin it.

Daniel Godfrey  
 Age: 21  
 Occupation: Student  
 From: Cardiff, Wales (living in Nottingham)  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: I made day 2 at PT Yokohama this year, and top 8 Welsh Nats.  
 Record in Standard: 4-2-1  
 Record in Draft: 6-1  
 What deck did you play in Standard and why? Perilous Storm because it has no terrible matchups, and nobody is playing any dedicated hate against storm any more. I was hoping for a lot of Angelfire and Solar Flare to be around, and I have a great matchup against control. The aggro matchups are a lot closer, but if I get a good draw I can't lose.  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? I liked the old Extended format, so in that respect I'm a little disappointed, but I've never played Legacy, so I guess I'll have to see.

Nick Lovett  
 Age: 24  
 From: Newport, Wales  
 Previous **Magic** Achievements: Semi-finals of Worlds 2006. Top eight of every multi-format event I've ever played in  
 Record in Standard: 4-1-2  
 Record in Draft: 6-1  
 What deck did you play and why? Rakdos because I was too chicken to play the [Walk the Aeons](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Walk+the+Aeons) combo deck  
 What are your thoughts on playing Legacy at Worlds? I have never even looked at a Legacy list. I guess I'll need to do that…

  

## Sunday, 10:55 a.m. - Quarterfinals: Simon O'Keeffe vs. Marco Orsini Jones

*by Richard Moore*

  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/db/f9/dbf99225ecf7b83718330216c9f1b10811a79e87c08e75c499f9c984380258c7.jpg)*Marco Orsini Jones*

As Simon and Marco sat down, Rakdos vs. [The Rack](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=The+Rack) deck, the last thing I expected the conversation to open with was questions about whether the rounds were timed or untimed??

"I don't think so but we all have trains to catch" - Simon

**Game 1 - Marco on the play**

A brief pause while the judge attempted to correct the shaky feature match table and the players were set to go. Both kept their opening hands.

The first play of the game was [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) by Marco, followed by a [Withered Wretch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Withered+Wretch) for Simon who passed the turn back. Confidant found a Rack and Marco sent a [Putrefy](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrefy) to the head of the [Withered Wretch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Withered+Wretch) swinging for 2. Simon returned the favour with a Hit on the Confidant and we were back to an empty board, Marco 2 life down courtesy of one of his lands.

Marco began the discard game with a [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) and a Rack, Simon discarding his [Urborg, Tomb of Yawgmoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Urborg%2C+Tomb+of+Yawgmoth) - no free black mana for Marco. Simon took a 2 point hit from his own land and swung in with a [Giant Solifuge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Solifuge), trading with the Rat with 3 points of trample for Marco.

Marco added a second Rack and a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) to the almost empty board. [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) cleared the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and Simon had a [Rakdos Carnarium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Carnarium), the perfect land considering it left him with 3 in hand.

Marco apparently decided that he had played enough spells and got in for 3 with his [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) before adding another, Simon just laid a [Keldon Megaliths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Keldon+Megaliths), which he probably didn't want to get to hellbent given the double Rack.

Simon had managed to gather 4 cards in his hand but was in some difficulty as the [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) continued to attack every turn. But Marco seemed unable to find any land to activate the other one. Simon played an unimpressive [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire) but looked fairly safe from the Racks with his 5 cards in hand.

The [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) continued to attack and Simon dropped to 2, then passed the turn straight back. Another activation of the village was met with a Hit from Simon, but Marco shrewdly elected to sacrifice a Rack rather than his lethal creature. Simon revealed his 5 card hand of all land.

Marco wins

to Marco

**Game 2 - Simon on the play**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e6/bb/e6bbb20b1f4817d92cbab61fac1519700ba44fa3de82b5450d41929e4eb040e4.jpg)*Simon O'Keeffe*

This time, Simon had the turn one play, the mighty [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic), but it seemed like he only had red mana sources as he laid a second [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) on turn 2. Marco made a [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats), removing a third [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) from Simon's hand.

Simon elected not to attack with the Fanatic and played a [Rakdos Carnarium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Carnarium), some black mana would be available next turn. Marco added an [Augur of Skulls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Augur+of+Skulls) to his rats, which also stayed home on defence.

Simon dropped a [Giant Solifuge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Solifuge) into play which joined the [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic) in the red zone. The rats blocked the solifuge and after damage, the Fanatic got rid of the Augur. Once again, the board was empty.

The game began to develop into a bit of a stare down as both players passed back and forth with only a single [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) cast targeting Marco. Marco finally broke it by playing a [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village), a [Bottle Gnomes](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bottle+Gnomes) and a Rack all in the same turn. Hit took care of the Rack and [Cruel Edict](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Edict) made Marco gain 3 life from the gnomes.

[Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) started the relentless attacks and was shortly joined by a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), a huge 5/6, missing only enchantments from being at his maximum for this matchup (no tribal cards here!). Double [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) was needed to take the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) down but since one had to be suspended, Simon was forced to take a hit from it and the Village, putting him down to 6.

Simon came back out swinging with a [Giant Solifuge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Solifuge) but unfortunately a bout of [Smallpox](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smallpox) sent him to an early grave. [Stupor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stupor) emptied Simon's hand and the village finished the job.

Marco wins

to Marco

**Game 3 - Simon on the play**

Both players dived back into their sideboard. The players had been given each others lists the night before and both players had tested their decks against what they expected the other to sideboard. Of course, this leads to all sorts of mind games and bluffs; are the cards that one player sideboards going to be the ones that they normally would or will they anticipate their opponent's sideboard strategy and adjust their deck to beat that? Who knows?

Through the relative silence, the banter from another quarterfinal, Stuart Wright vs Dave Grant, could be clearly heard.

An aggressive start for Simon in this game, [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic) followed by [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant). Marco summoned a [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats), which got promptly removed by the goblin. Confidant revealed another Confidant and Simon dropped the second one on to the board.  
 Putrify removed one of the Confidants but the second one revealed a third [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant)!! A second Putrify kept the Bob Maher invitational card population down. Simon passed the turn back and Marco tried to slow the pace a bit with an [Augur of Skulls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Augur+of+Skulls). Simon thought it was a [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) and started to discard [Hit/Run](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hit%2FRun). Marco looked a bit confused and pointed out it was an Augur.

"Oh, err, I thought it was a rat…I mean, I was just getting ahead of the game and I'm going to discard it to the [Cry of Contrition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cry+of+Contrition) you are about to play" Simon stated unconvincingly.

Marco smiled, tapped his remaining black mana and did indeed cast [Cry of Contrition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cry+of+Contrition) ("See!" - Simon) but when he tried to haunt the Augur, Simon had the [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) ready. Finally Confidant hit a land, much to the relief of Simon who had managed to get himself down to 10 from his own lands and Confidants, and Solifuge joined him in the red zone. Marco tried another [Stupor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stupor) and a [Cry of Contrition](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cry+of+Contrition) but when [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) was revealed during Simon's next upkeep, he simply scooped up his cards.

"Too many confidants"

Simon wins

2-1 to Marco

**Game 4 - Marco on the play**

Simon started the game suspending a [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) after Marco had laid an [Overgrown Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Overgrown+Tomb) tapped. [Augur of Skulls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Augur+of+Skulls) was the first creature to the party who was the target of [Demonfire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demonfire) before he ever got to see an upkeep, the [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) having been aimed directly at Marco.

Turn 3, [Call of the Herd](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+of+the+Herd) made its first appearance in the match, its first elephant removed by [Cruel Edict](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Edict) and its second by [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate). [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) was activated but Simon had the [Hit/Run](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hit%2FRun) ready for it. Marco had a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) which was a 3/4 but chose to block Simon's [Giant Solifuge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Giant+Solifuge).

Simon seemed to have all the answers but Marco had another [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and a [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) to go with it. Simon untapped and made a [Mortivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mortivore)

"How big is he?" asked Marco

"HUGE!!....2/2"

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cd/93/cd9320e52fd8e43abf7201a65c66a579363379d760511f5eb1b6d3d04fdf49d9.jpg)

Marco untapped looked at the board, looked at his hand and cast [Smallpox](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smallpox). He elected to sacrifice a swamp, his [Ravenous Rats](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravenous+Rats) and discarded down to 1 card, leaving him with a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) in play. Simon was not so lucky. He said goodbye to his last card in hand, one of his lands and his one lone [Mortivore](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mortivore) despite his joking attempts to regenerate. So Simon looked like he was going to have to topdeck to beat the [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) but he had a while to do it. But then Marco decided to play the last remaining card in his hand, [The Rack](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=The+Rack)!

So Simon had the choice of keeping the cards he drew and taking less from [The Rack](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=The+Rack) but damage from the 4/5 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) or playing them to try and stop the creature but dying faster to [The Rack](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=The+Rack). He struggled on for a couple more turns but realised that it was a bit of a lose-lose situation and scooped up his cards.

**Marco Orsini Jones wins the game and the match beating Simon O'Keeffe 3-1**

  

## Sunday, 11:43 a.m. - Quarterfinals: Dave Grant vs. Stu Wright

*by Keith Spragg*

 

Dave Grant arrived at the table looking like he's carrying two suitcases with him - only these ones were under his eyes. He had somewhat of a late night last night apparently, and I think it might not have just been testing. But, with all the good will in the world, we're talking about Dave "The Great White Hype" Grant here - Granty is a man full of quips, and he talks to his cards like they were his friends.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/fc/d0/fcd0d43e5dda859630e3872a518cf56e02ae1f300ced2d936155daae299bf3cc.jpg)*Dave Grant*

His opponent in the meantime is somewhat more composed, wearing his bright green **Magic** *Online* clan t-shirt, but still full of smiles. Stuart Wright is a deckbuilder of world-renown, and is cool calm and collected, for now.

Stuart kicks things off by revealing that he had a plan: "Hey Dave, I think I need those cards I borrowed from you back…."  
 Dave retorts: "You can't! The Hype must continue!"

Stu asked the judge which zone of the playmat he should use when the Graveyard spills out of control thanks to all the cards he's hoping to put there from Dredge.

**Game 1**

Dave was on the play and kept his 7, and Stuart likewise kept his 7 and reached out to put something in play. Dave was incredulous, after all he was supposed to be playing first, but Stuart revealed it to be a [Gemstone Caverns](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemstone+Caverns) - The Luckyman's Paradise out in force! Stuart put a Flamekin Zealot in the removed from Game Zone.

Stuart couldn't make best use of the Cavern however, as he laid a land and played a [Llanowar Mentor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Mentor) - the discard outlet that doesn't take long to assemble an army if he's left to his own devices. [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) was deployed by Dave to take down the troublesome man.

Another Man, another burn as [Bonded Fetch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonded+Fetch) was introduced to the Graveyard by a [Hit/Run](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hit%2FRun). [Bonded Fetch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonded+Fetch) did however manage to put a [Stinkweed Imp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stinkweed+Imp) in to the Graveyard first, which dredges on the next turn, finding Narcomeba and a Bridge on the way.

Stu introduced [Magus of the Bazaar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Bazaar) and [Drowned Rusalka](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Drowned+Rusalka) to the board, and Dave's plan of burning each and every monster the second they arrive looked like it was under fire.  
[Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire) and a [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) join Dave's side of the board however, and [Drowned Rusalka](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Drowned+Rusalka) was burnt at the stake, giving Stuart a 2/2 from [Bridge from Below](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bridge+from+Below).

On turn three, and Stuart managed to accumulate a sum of 14 cards in his Graveyard already. Grave-Troll had been dredged to reveal another Grave-Troll which was summarily [Dread Return](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread+Return)ed. A 9/9 Grave-Troll spelled bad times for the Hype, and after the [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) unsuspended to take a smiling Stuart to 12, which is all too much for Dave to finish off.

Stuart 1-0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/a0/14/a01429ef2e3f019277d714a0f35da466f048ef44d23e656e1605f091b7a6dcdc.jpg)*Stuart Wright*

"Your plan of burning all my men away looked good, until I got to keep one of them after all, and that's baaaaaad for you!"

Seeing Stu do the Happy Dance is quite a sight.

"I'll play, Stu" - "Are you Sure? I'll just [Gemstone Caverns](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemstone+Caverns) again…."

Amazingly, a Bee decides to fly in the window, and lands directly on Dave's orange sleeves: "It's a SIGN!" Proclaimed the Hype…

**Game 2**

Dave kept, while Stu had to mulligan.

It was just a land for Dave on Turn one, while Stuart counjured up a Lannowar Mentor. It was burnt, of course, and Stuart tried a [Magus of the Bazaar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Bazaar). This met a [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic), while Stu followed this up with ANOTHER Magus. This was turning out to be quite a spicy Mulligan, all things considered.

The second Magus is met by a [Demonfire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demonfire), reaveled by a Bob that Dave sneaked down the turn before, and Dave's trump card of [Withered Wretch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Withered+Wretch) hit the table. Dave's new plan was to keep the important cards out of the graveyard, putting Stuart under pressure all the while.

Dave was sat forward at the table this game, he was in the ascendancy having the ability to burn all the monsters and remove all the cards from the graveyard. Stuart knew it - and folded on the spot when Bob reveals a Fanatic and not the [Hit/Run](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hit%2FRun) that Stu was hoping for.

Total Number of Happy Dances this game - Zero.

1-1

Between games, Dave suggested that he was going to [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate), [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate), [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char), [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char), [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char), [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) Stu this game.

**Game 3**

Both players kept their hands for the game, and a turn 2 [Magus of the Bazaar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Bazaar) from Stu elicited "How Bazaar" \[sic\] from Dave - while the Magus became very afraid of a sexy black bordered [Terror](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terror).

"Narcomeba? [Llanowar Mentor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Mentor)?" posed the questioning Stu. Another [Terror](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terror), this time for the discard outlet.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b9/7f/b97f4bb9e3b37fde9b75cb0e8d2408522582880e39361a3b8349a00ecaebebe2.jpg)

[Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) and a SECOND Narcomoebea hit the table, while the Great One showed up to the table for an elated Dave.  
[Merfolk Looter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merfolk+Looter) also joined the table for Stuart - "They're all gonna die, Stu. They're ALL gonna die."

Dave [Demonfire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demonfire)d the looter, and [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char)red the [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) after damage - [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic) and Bob hit the Red Zone, so the pesky wall followed the way of the other monsters.

"Hit Hit Hit Hit Hit!!" Chants Stu, hoping to see 8 points of damage from Bob. It was a land.

[Martyr of Ashes](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martyr+of+Ashes) made Dave look like he was no longer the Hype - he quite probably was going to kill all the monsters!

The Pair of Narcomoebeas are actually doing damage though - Dave was at 10 - and Grave-Troll joined the board as a 4/4: "How Much?!" quips Dave amazed at the size of the Troll - "You HAVE killed four of my men, after all!". It was all irrelevant, though, [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) and [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic) took down the Grave-Troll, and Weathered Wretch also hit the board…

"Hit Hit Hit Hit Hit" Chants Stu, again - it was another land.

Brownscale showed up on Stu's side, and the fact was beginning to sink in to the Great White Hype's sleep deprived brain that he could lose this to a pair of 1/1s. Dave was at 6, and worried about being killed by his own Bob, so sends it on a lone suicide mission - the Brownscale takes it down, but Dave can't follow up with any burn to take the pesky wall equivalent away - This meant that Stu was on the attack! The Brownscale joined the two Narcomebas in the red Zone.

"Joy! You're going to die to the 'moebas!" proclaimed Stu, and maybe Merfolk looter is going to attack....

The Judge leaned in - was there a problem? [Sulfurous Springs](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sulfurous+Springs) had been tapped to produce a second black for [Withered Wretch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Withered+Wretch), and that put Dave on one less life - Dave couldn't win from here, and entered the scoop phase.

2-1 to Stuart

Dave gave himself a good talking to at this point, and repeatedly told himself to wake up.

"Is the Hype dying?" asked a member of the audience - "Never!"

**Game 4**

Another [Gemstone Caverns](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemstone+Caverns) hit the table ahead of the normal curve of the game, much to Dave's disappointment. Dave tried to get some of that momentum back and played a [Martyr of Ashes](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martyr+of+Ashes) out…

…which let Stuart cast a [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast) before his first turn! The [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast) also let Stu dredge three cards, and Stu was clearly feeling at a huge advantage.

The next monster from Dave was a [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic), which was met by the dredged [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast), and Stu dropped a [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle) on the table, naming his nemesis, [Withered Wretch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Withered+Wretch). A [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba) hit the table from dredging the [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast) again, and Dave felt like it was time to drop his hand on the table - Bob, Mogg and Martyr hit the table, but things were looking grim in the face of [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast), as all three of these monsters are afflicted by only having one toughness. Indeed, Bob hit the bin, and dredging this back showed [Llanowar Mentor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Mentor), [Stinkweed Imp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stinkweed+Imp) and a second [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast), much to the chagrin of Dave.

"Stinky!" exclaimed a dejected Dave, as Stu dredged the perpetual monster back. The Graveyard looked huge, with 18 cards in it, including hits such as Grave-Troll, [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam), and [Golgari Brownscale](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Brownscale).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6f/f4/6ff44ab1698dde31ec427a06f387ef3e011db790787e4bdd63195f573dbf3037.jpg)

"You're up buddy!" said Dave, and slumped in the Chair - the Crowd are expectant of another Happy Dance here, and Stuart was sat up lively in his chair, ready to give the crowd what they wanted.

In came the [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba) again, with Stu having picked up [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam) from the dredge that turn.

"I don't wanna die!" says Dave, pretending to be [Martyr of Ashes](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martyr+of+Ashes), as [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast) took the red mini-wrath down.

Brownscale came back to Stu's hand, and Stu practically jumped in the seat as Dave slumped back in the chair again.

One quick dredge later and Troll hit the table as an ENOURMOUS 13/13, but is [Terror](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terror)ed end of turn, allowing Dave to use his Megaliths. 15 life each, but this time, the Troll came back as a 16/16 this time.

The hand extended from Granty - Stu's prediction that he was going to go down in a hail of flame is proved wrong - "What Bracket am I in again?" asked Stu.

3-1 to Stuart Wright, and the Hype can come back next year…

  

## Sunday, 1:21 p.m. - Quarterfinals: Eduardo Sajgalik vs. Craig Jones

*by Tim Willoughby*

 

For our top eight here at the Great British Nationals, there is not a lot in the way of counter **Magic**. Between all 8 decks there are 8 [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand)s, 3 Vensers and 2 [Draining Whelk](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Draining+Whelk)s. Most of those are in this matchup, while burn is everywhere.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3e/08/3e08bf41b8ebc67401df9e9e5098aa47efd9b795ff9669ca151fc4b4a260ba3c.jpg)

Flying the flag for England is Craig Jones, whose carefully reasoned plan for his matchup against Eduardo Sajgalik was to burn his opponent's face off. Eduardo, the Frenchman who is over here as a student, and qualified on rating, will be looking to do tricksy coming into play plans with his [Momentary Blink](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Momentary+Blink) deck.

Craig won the roll, and after a mulligan, he suspended a turn one [Greater Gargadon](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Greater+Gargadon), followed by a Mogg War-Marshal for turn 2. While all Eduardo was doing was casting a Signet, Craig was starting to bash. He paid echo and swung for two. First blood!

On the next attack, Eduardo had a [Venser, Shaper Savant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Venser%2C+Shaper+Savant) to bounce Craig's [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village), but before it could block it was burned away. Craig then played a [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire) and passed. The Frenchman played a land, signet and passed. Craig just left his goblins in the red zone, and passed. Sajgalik had an Aethermage's Touch, and hit a [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate), who tried to bounce a Goblin token (ably represented by a Craig Jones Pro Player card). The Goblin got eaten by [Greater Gargadon](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Greater+Gargadon), and the Cloudskate ate a [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire), before Craig suspended a [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) and passed. Sajgalik was now on 15, which Craig deemed to be far too much for his liking.

While Sajgalik was playing draw-go, Craig continued to shoot for the damage. His [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) put Eduardo to 12, before [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) and [Mogg War Marshal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+War+Marshal)l came in. [Venser, Shaper Savant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Venser%2C+Shaper+Savant) jumped in, and Craig thought long and hard after his [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) had been bounced, about if he had effects before blocks. He did not, and [Mogg War Marshal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+War+Marshal) turned into another Craig Jones token.

Sajgalik played a [Skeletal Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skeletal+Vampire) and passed. It got into play with it's Bats unimpeded, but they couldn't save it from [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) at the end of turn. Craig played an old school Legends [Pendelhaven](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pendelhaven), and sacrificed sufficient land to get [Greater Gargadon](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Greater+Gargadon) into play. It got to beat up a bat before [Angel of Despair](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Despair) killed it off. Suddenly Craig was on a clock. [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) from Craig got hit by [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand), then [Momentary Blink](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Momentary+Blink) allowed Sajgalik to kill off a [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) with his angel.

Eduardo tried to finish things off with an Aethermage's Touch after the end of turn trigger. He hit a [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate), and bounced one of Craig's lands. Attacks took Jones to 3. Blink on the Angel destroyed Craig's [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), at which point it was all academic.

Eduardo Sajgalik wins game 1.

**Game 2**

For game 2, each player dived to their sideboards. Craig took out [Greater Gargadon](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Greater+Gargadon)s and War Marshal's to bring in 3 [Magus of the Moon](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Moon) and 3 [Tin Street Hooligan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tin+Street+Hooligan). Eduardo took out [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand)s, Grand Arbiters, an Angel, and a [Dimir Aqueduct](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dimir+Aqueduct) to bring in 4 [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher), 3 [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God) and 2 [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle).

In game 2, it was Eduardo who had the mulligans, two of them in total. Having tested the matchup the previous evening, Craig had thought that the Aethermage's Touch deck could stumble with slow hands, and these were where the red deck shines.

Jones led with [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic), and had a 0/1 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) for turn two. Eduardo suspended a [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate) for his turn, while Craig played and cycled a [Horizon Canopy](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horizon+Canopy) such that he could attack for 2, before suspending a [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt).

The Frenchman was on the back foot. He played [Court Hussar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Court+Hussar), without any white mana up, leaving himself as the only target for the bolt, and meaning that [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) was even bigger. With a [Pendelhaven](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pendelhaven) for his turn, Craig got to swing for 5 in total. Sajgalik was pulling back though. He had a [Hallowed Fountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Fountain) and an [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher) the following turn. It was all over anyway though as Craig had a [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) to pump his [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and knock down Sajgalik, then enough combat damage to end things

Craig Jones wins game 2, making it 1-1 in the series

**Game 3**

For game 3, Sajgalik removed a couple of [Court Hussar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Court+Hussar), and two [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God), going closer to his original main deck. Grand Arbiter came back, while [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) remained out.

"So… do you want the extra card Mr Control Deck?"

Eduardo was not biting. On the play he kept his hand, while again Craig was on the mulligans.

Each player had a land that came into play tapped, but [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle) stopped Craig's [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) from doing anything more than tapping for green. Craig didn't seem to mind too much though, as he had a [Keldon Marauders](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Keldon+Marauders) that her rather wanted to turn into a [Lava Axe](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lava+Axe). Eduardo played a [Godless Shrine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Godless+Shrine) untapped and cast [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher). The Marauders successfully bashed, and Craig continued to play creatures that make life awkward, with [Troll Ascetic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Troll+Ascetic) entering play.

When [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher) attacked, the life totals went to 16 each, until [Keldon Marauders](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Keldon+Marauders) lived their dream, dying to become a 2 mana [Lava Axe](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lava+Axe). [Troll Ascetic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Troll+Ascetic) went into the red zone, to which Sajgalik played Aethermage's Touch. He found another [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher) and a [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate). After a little thought, he put the Vanishing flyer into play, going back up to 17 before taking some Troll beats. Craig suspended a [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) and passed. The life totals went to 12 each when Sajgalik played an untapped *Ravnica* dual land, before casting [Grand Arbiter Augustin IV](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grand+Arbiter+Augustin+IV). Craig had to pay one extra for his [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) on upkeep, and was nearly thwarted by [Momentary Blink](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Momentary+Blink), but for another [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) in hand. [Troll Ascetic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Troll+Ascetic) continued his good work in the red zone.

Sajgalik had another Blink for his Riftwatcher, followed by another Riftwatcher for his turn, jumping up to 17 before passing. With 2 [Momentary Blink](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Momentary+Blink)s in the graveyard, and a pair of [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher)s to use them on, Sajgalik was functionally on a great deal of life, making life pretty tough for Jones. He had a Tin-Street Hooligan to destroy [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle), and a [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire).

Each player was down to low cards in hand , and on 7 from a lot of Riftwatcher swings, Craig had to get aggressive fast. He activated a [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village), and attacked with his team, commanding his opponent to take 8. At this point, Craig had dealt 30 damage in the game, but his opponent was still on 6. [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire) functionally made it four, but the various Blinks made it quite a bit more, and Craig was on a 2 turn clock himself.

Sajgalik tried to blink a Riftwatcher, but Craig had the [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) ready to kill it in response. [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God) dealt with Tin-Street Hooligan and [Troll Ascetic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Troll+Ascetic) and took Sajgalik to 10. Sajgalik had just one card left, to Craig's two, with some effective damage sources on the board in the form of [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire) and a pair of [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village)s, though he was one mana short of being able to make both. Craig had another [Troll Ascetic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Troll+Ascetic), and a [Horizon Canopy](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horizon+Canopy) to animate one of his Villages with, allowing him to attack Eduardo to 7. In spite of all that life-gain, the Frenchman's tricks had not been enough to stop the red spells.

"Red spells are the most powerful spells in the world!!!" laughed Craig, living up to his evil Genius persona.

"This isn't best of three is it?"

**Game 4**  
 For the next game Sajgalik had still not settled on his side boarding plan. He took out the final [Wrath of God](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wrath+of+God), and a [Skeletal Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skeletal+Vampire), to bring back his [Court Hussar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Court+Hussar)s.

![char](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=char)

There were no mulligans on either side, and while Eduarodo had no early gas, Craig had a turn one [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic), and a turn two [Keldon Marauders](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Keldon+Marauders). Sajgalik tried to offer some blue vanishing counters for the 3/3 Warriors, but Craig sneered and reached for his red beads. It was the classic Red vs. Blue battle, and Prof wanted to make it clear which side of the fence he was on.

Eduardo had a [Court Hussar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Court+Hussar), which blocked [Mogg Fanatic](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mogg+Fanatic), and then died to a [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire). Another [Keldon Marauders](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Keldon+Marauders) came down, dropping Sajgalik to 13. He played another [Court Hussar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Court+Hussar), still on just 3 land, and passed. Marauder #1 died, taking Sajgalik to 12, while Marauder #2 beat up [Court Hussar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Court+Hussar). Craig played a 2/3 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and passed.

Sajgalik had no plays for his turn, and at the end of turn, Craig used his [Horizon Canopy](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horizon+Canopy) to draw a card. [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) came in, but was bounced back to Craig's hand by [Venser, Shaper Savant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Venser%2C+Shaper+Savant). The lhurgoyf came back, and Craig passed after playing a [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village).

Eduardo played [Grand Arbiter Augustin IV](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grand+Arbiter+Augustin+IV) and passed with 2 mana up. With it's cost reduction abilities, Aethermage's Touch , but missed , forcing Sajgalik to block with Venser. As Sajgalik was tapped out, Craig took the opportunity to do the same to cast [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) and kill the Azorius legend.

[Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher) was the next creature for Sajgalik, going back up to 11. [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) and [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) came in, prompting another Aethermage's Touch from Eduardo. A [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate) popped out, bouncing [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and joining [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher) in the double block on [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village). Craig killed off [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher), then post combat [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) came back.

Another [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher) took Sajgalik to 15. This was going to be a tough race for Craig. He was down to 2 cards in hand, and when he attacked with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), he faced a block before a Venser to bounce [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher). Craig had another [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), but the life totals were going in the wrong direction for Craig's liking.

The second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) was bounced by [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate), and Eduardo snuck in for 2. A Tin-Street Hooligan destroyed a signet for Craig, and [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) to the Cloudskate removed the last blocker, allowing [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) to swing for 6. Sajgalik untapped into a [Skeletal Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skeletal+Vampire). The life totals were 11 to 10 in Sajgalik's favour, and he now had bats to block with. After killing off a bat in just this fashion, Craig replayed his second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and passed.

Eduardo swung Craig down to six in the air, and played an [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher), to go up to 13. Craig went into the red zone, and found an Angel appearing thanks to Aethermage's Touch. [Angel of Despair](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Despair) killed a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), and Craig scooped in the face of a lethal attack.

**Game 5**

For game 5, Eduardo cut down on Grand Arbiters, and brought back a [Skeletal Vampire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Skeletal+Vampire). He also traded a [Dimir Aqueduct](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dimir+Aqueduct) for a Wrather of God. After 4 tense games, it would all come down to one deciding game.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ed/f9/edf90b7885c837d32bb847b0cf56ecfb21a44456d5a7379423e09dcd3f71075d.jpg)

Both players kept, and Craig led with a suspended [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt). He followed up with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) on turn two, to which Eduardo suspended a [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate). Craig got in for a cheeky point, before playing a [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire) and passing.

Both players were playing with just 2 lands, which seemed to favour Craig, even though there were soon 2 [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate)s suspended. Craig played an [Incinerate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incinerate) and the [Seal of Fire](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seal+of+Fire) targeting his opponent, and cracked in with the now 3 power [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). Only 8 points left to do.

A [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle) came down naming [Horizon Canopy](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Horizon+Canopy), but Craig seemed unphased. He attacked his opponent down to 5, and faced the first [Riftwing Cloudskate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Riftwing+Cloudskate), who bounced [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf). A [Godless Shrine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Godless+Shrine) allowed an [Aven Riftwatcher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aven+Riftwatcher), and in his Craig's upkeep [Rift Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rift+Bolt) took Eduardo to 2. A land off the top allowed Craig to cast [Char](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Char) for the win.

Craig Jones wins 3-2, and takes the final slot on the Great Britain National Team!

  

## Sunday, 2:33 p.m. - Semifinals: Stuart Wright vs. Daniel Godfrey

*by Staff*

  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/6d/9b/6d9b76f7a75f7e3761396576e7dceab8e96e0a55debeee053ca192aa15ce4e29.jpg)*Daniel Godfrey*

As both players took their seats, Stuart seemed quite upbeat about the match.

"It could be worse, I could be playing against the other Rakdos deck. I think I would be pushing my luck to win that match up again."

So Dredge (Stuart) vs. Perilous Storm (Daniel), game plans as different as they could be. Daniel hoping to cast as many spells as possible to power out his powerful storm spells, [Grapeshot](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grapeshot), [Empty the Warrens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empty+the+Warrens) or [Ignite Memories](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ignite+Memories). Stuart, on the other hand, wants to fill up his graveyard, playing minimal spells and then hope to either win with a single huge creature, like a Golgari Gravetroll or a bunch of [Bridge from Below](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bridge+from+Below) zombie tokens.

The topic of the pregame conversation was Legacy replacing Extended at worlds. We now know that there will be a whole day of Legacy play, and neither had really played it a great deal up to that point. With a deckbuilder like Stuart on the team though, it had to mean that the GB team would be in good stead.

**Game 1 - Stuart on the Play**

Stuart chose to keep his hand, Daniel was not so happy and quickly mulliganed. Stuart began to wonder aloud whether his opponents looking at their hands with unhappy looks on their faces was a bluff and they actually had a good hand but Daniel proved that theory wrong as he mulliganed a second time down to 5 cards.

"Ah well, I think I lose the first game anyway"

Stuart opened the plays with a turn 2 [Magus of the Bazaar](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Magus+of+the+Bazaar) which received a [Grapeshot](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grapeshot) from Daniel. As Daniel reached for his pen to write down the loss of life incurred from his [Shivan Reef](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shivan+Reef) for the red mana, he accidentally flipped over the top card of his deck, revealing a [Grapeshot](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grapeshot). Judge was called over and the deck was randomized with Daniel receiving a caution.

"I wanted to draw that one too…"

Stuart, not deterred by the death of the first, untapped and made another Magus. This time Daniel had the [Repeal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Repeal). For the third turn in a row, Stuart made a Magus and this time it survived. Daniel cast [Hatching Plans](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hatching+Plans) for his turn and passed.

At last Stuart was able to get some cards into the graveyard. The Magus put a [Stinkweed Imp](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stinkweed+Imp) and a [Bridge from Below](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bridge+from+Below) there, then the dredge added another Bridge and a Grave-Troll. [Bonded Fetch](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonded+Fetch) joined the Magus and Stuart's graveyard threatened to fall off the play mat. He now had 2 Bridges and the [Flame-Kin Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame-Kin+Zealot) but no third creature to win that turn.

Daniel seemed to have the answer, at least temporarily, with [Claws of Gix](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Claws+of+Gix) and [Empty the Warrens](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Empty+the+Warrens) for 4 goblins. He could now sacrifice a token and remove the 2 Bridges if Stuart tried to flashback [Dread Return](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread+Return) meaning that he would get no zombies.

As Stuart dredged and hit the third Bridge, it started to look like he was going to have to go with the non-Bridge strategy for victory. Further dredging meant that Stuart had 25 cards in his graveyard and still hadn't hit a single [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba) but had now collected all 4 [Bridge from Below](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bridge+from+Below). Almost certainly having to abandon the Bridge plan, Stuart cast [Life from the Loam](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Life+from+the+Loam) to get some lands back gathering his land for the big creatures. He made a third creature in the form of a [Drowned Rusalka](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Drowned+Rusalka) and flashed back [Dread Return](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dread+Return) for a [Golgari Grave-Troll](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Golgari+Grave-Troll). Daniel responded by killing a token and removing the 4 copies of [Bridge from Below](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bridge+from+Below) and Stuart put a 14/14 Grave-Troll into play.

Daniel untapped, sacrificed the [Hatching Plans](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hatching+Plans) to get some more cards and passed the turn. Stuart began the long process of attacking his Grave-Troll into Daniels board of 3 remaining goblin tokens so that he could start picking away his life total. He then made a [Merfolk Looter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merfolk+Looter) and a [Llanowar Mentor](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Llanowar+Mentor), the Looter receiving a [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand).

Daniel seemed to be doing little but making lands. He suspended a [Lotus Bloom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Bloom) and passed the turn back to Stuart. However, Stuart now had the mana to activate [Svogthos, the Restless Tomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Svogthos%2C+the+Restless+Tomb) and Daniel had only 1 goblin. Stuart attacked with both, one getting through. Daniel drew one more card and gave it up.

Stuart wins

to Stuart

**Game 2 - Daniel on the play**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d9/8f/d98fa1d63a562433e4fe80a0000f100abcb4202865ef8943edcb832e64942583.jpg)*Stuart Wright's Happy Dance*

As they sideboarded, the 2 players spoke about how the previous game had played out.

"Aren't you only playing, like, 18 land or something?" asked Stuart

"Yeah, I drew most of them"

"I didn't think I would win game 1 with Grave-Troll, I'm not supposed to beat you with the Troll"

Stuart tried to talk Daniel into going second, stating that all going first would do was make his Gemstone Cavern better. However, Daniel didn't bite and kept his hand going first. Stuart glanced at his and shuffled it back in.

"I had a [Gemstone Mine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemstone+Mine), I guess I could have tried and sneaked that into play but I don't think I would have got away with it.

Stuart's second hand did have a [Gemstone Caverns](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gemstone+Caverns) but it seemed that that was all it had going for it and he threw that one away too, mulliganing to 5 which he kept.

The first play of the game was Daniel's turn 2 [Hatching Plans](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hatching+Plans), Stuart made a Magus of Bazaar and passed the turn back. Daniel laid a third land and allowed the Magus to untap and start doing its thing. Stuart used it during his upkeep and discarded 2 lands and a [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba), no dredge cards yet it seemed. He added a second Magus to the board At the end of turn, Daniel executed the [Perilous Research](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Perilous+Research) / [Hatching Plans](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hatching+Plans) and drew 5 cards. He then cast a [Martyr of Ashes](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martyr+of+Ashes) and revealed a [Rite of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rite+of+Flame) to kill the Magus team.

"Right then, beatdown time" announced Stuart as he put a [Narcomoeba](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Narcomoeba) into play. Daniel smiled and played a [Hatching Plans](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hatching+Plans) and a [Claws of Gix](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Claws+of+Gix), passing the turn back. Daniel then cycled a [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) on Stuart's [Drowned Rusalka](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Drowned+Rusalka) and drew yet more cards by sacrificing the [Hatching Plans](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hatching+Plans) to the Claws. Stuart looked on with a mere 1 card in his hand as Daniel's hand began to resemble Stuart's graveyard from the first game.

On Daniel's turn, he laid a land, took a while looking at his hand, and cast [Rite of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20170719033333/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rite+of+Flame) announcing "Storm count of 1".
